\begin{tikzpicture}[x=1cm,y=1cm,font=\small,>=stealth,
  job/.style={circle,draw,fill=white,minimum size=6.8mm,inner sep=0pt}]
  \draw[black!55,rounded corners=2pt] (-.15,-.25) rectangle (10.75,2.85);
  \node[anchor=west] at (-.15,3.13) {Parent $W$};
  \node at (4,3.13) {Child $W'$};
  \node at (9.1,3.13) {$b'\subset W$, $|b'|=3$};

  \fill[black!5] (8.55,-.12) rectangle (9.65,2.72);
  \foreach \yy/\ii/\xx/\cc in {2.2/1/3.55/blue!65!black,1.3/2/4/teal!65!black,.4/3/4.45/violet!70!black} {
    \draw[draw=\cc,fill=\cc!5,rounded corners=3pt]
      (.03,\yy-.34) rectangle (10.57,\yy+.34);
    \node[text=\cc] at (1.05,\yy) {$I_{\ii}\cap W$};
    \node[text=\cc] at (2.5,\yy) {$\cdots$};
    \node[job,draw=\cc] (x\ii) at (\xx,\yy) {$x_{\ii}$};
    \node[job,draw=\cc] (z\ii) at (9.1,\yy) {$z_{\ii}$};
    \draw[->,draw=\cc] (x\ii) -- node[above,font=\footnotesize] {$\prec$} (z\ii);
    \node[text=\cc] at (10.05,\yy) {$\cdots$};
  }
  \draw[dashed,black!60,rounded corners=2pt] (3.03,-.12) rectangle (4.98,2.72);
  \draw[black!60,rounded corners=2pt] (8.55,-.12) rectangle (9.65,2.72);

    {$x_i\in I_i,\ x_i\prec z_i\ \Longrightarrow\ z_i\in I_i$
     };
  \node[anchor=east,font=\footnotesize] at (10.75,-.64)
    {$I_i\cap I_j\cap W=\varnothing\ (i\ne j)\ \Longrightarrow\ z_i\ne z_j$};
\end{tikzpicture}

Three horizontal rows represent three injections $I_1, I_2, I_3$:
- Row 1: A rounded rectangle labeled $I_1 \cap W$, followed by dots $\dots$, connecting to a circular node $x_1$, which connects via an arrow labeled $\prec$ to a circular node $z_1$, followed by dots $\dots$.
- Row 2: A rounded rectangle labeled $I_2 \cap W$, followed by dots $\dots$, connecting to a circular node $x_2$, which connects via an arrow labeled $\prec$ to a circular node $z_2$, followed by dots $\dots$.
- Row 3: A rounded rectangle labeled $I_3 \cap W$, followed by dots $\dots$, connecting to a circular node $x_3$, which connects via an arrow labeled $\prec$ to a circular node $z_3$, followed by dots $\dots$.
A vertical rounded container encloses $\{x_1, x_2, x_3\}$ labeled "Child $W'$".

<a id="pm-h-20"></a>
#### Flattening the history of a run

Only the first entry of a switch pair can carry a complicated predicate from an earlier run. The following lemma removes it whenever we need to reconstruct an old cutoff on a descendant parent.

<a id="stmt-lem-flatten"></a>
<div id="lem:flatten" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:flatten"></a>
**Lemma 19** (Local flattening). *Suppose a run began with a switch out of a node $Q_0$, using left boundary $a_0$ in the resulting run frame, and entries $$G_0\cap\{\lambda\ge h_0\},\qquad D_{a_0},
 \qquad Q_0\setminus D_{a_0}\subseteq G_0 .$$ Let $U\subseteq Q_0$ be a later parent in that run. If the entry derived from the first displayed entry survives into an unpruned carry out of $U$, write its current form as $G_0\cap\{\lambda\ge k\}$. Then the carried portion of this pair has the same union on $U$ as the global upset $$D_{a_0}\cup\{\lambda\ge k\}.
 \label{eq:flatten}$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-flatten"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-flatten</summary>

*Proof.* Track the original entries through later reindexings. As long as the first entry survives, a suffix operation cannot trim or discard the second entry: a cutoff at that later index would already discard the first, permanently. Thus the second entry is either still carried untrimmed, or was pruned because it was empty on some intermediate node containing $U$. In the latter case $D_{a_0}\cap U=\varnothing$.

On $U\cap D_{a_0}$, the second entry, when this set is nonempty, therefore selects every job. Every job of $U\setminus D_{a_0}$ lies in $G_0$, because $U\subseteq Q_0$. On that remainder the first entry selects exactly the rank suffix $\lambda\ge k$. This proves equality of the two unions on $U$. Both factors in <a href="#eq:flatten" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:flatten">[eq:flatten]</a> are upsets, so their union is globally upward closed, although it need not agree with the carried pair outside $U$. ◻

</details>

A new switch out of a node $W$ uses the edge $U\to W$ by which $W$ was created. Let $H_-$ be that incoming edge’s low cutoff, in its own working frame, and reserve $S$ for the cutoff of the new outgoing edge. We need an extension of $H_-$ that does not retain a complicated switch predicate from an earlier run. Local flattening provides it using lists and covering properties belonging to already constructed ancestors.

<a id="stmt-lem-old-low"></a>
<div id="lem:old-low" class="lemma">

**Lemma 20** (A short extension of an earlier low set). *Consider an already constructed edge from a parent $U$ to a child $W$, in its old working frame. Its low cutoff $H_-\subseteq U$ has a global downset extension $H_-^*$ agreeing with it on $U$. This extension can be chosen with at most $21$ leaves.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-old-low"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-old-low</summary>

*Proof.* Use the boundary notation of $U$ in that old frame for this proof. If the edge was a switch relative to the tag of $U$, its low cutoff is a rank prefix of $U\setminus D_a$. Thus $$(J\setminus D_a)\cap\{\lambda\le r\}$$ is the required global downset, using at most two leaves.

If the edge was same, its low cutoff on $U$ equals $P_b$ minus its unpruned carry union, without the fresh injection. The old list on $U$ has at most five entries by [lem:list-length](#lem:list-length). If its run began at the root, all carry entries are simple: the root base or injections, possibly rank-trimmed. If it began at a switch, the only possibly complicated carry entry is the first of that switch pair. If it is present in the carry, replace the carried pair by <a href="#eq:flatten" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:flatten">[eq:flatten]</a>; if it is absent from the carry, every carried entry is already simple.

This gives a global upset agreeing with the carry union on $U$. It has at most five terms, each with at most four leaves. Indeed an injection with a rank trim has three leaves; a root base or a second switch base with a trim has at most two; and the flattened pair has two. Taking $P_b$ minus this union gives a global downset with at most $1+5\cdot4=21$ leaves. An empty carry is represented by false. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-21"></a>
#### Constructing the new switch upset

<a id="eq:ancestry"></a>
Suppose a switch out of $W$ produces a nonempty child. By [lem:edge-labels](#lem:edge-labels), $W$ is not the root. Let $U$ be the parent that produced $W$. That preceding edge was taken in the tag frame of $W$, which is opposite to the current working frame. In the current frame write $a_-,b_-$ for the boundaries of $U$ and $v_-$ for its center. The temporal arrangement is $$a_-<v_-\le a<b\le b_-.
 \label{eq:ancestry}$$ Here $a$ is an actual ending endpoint of that preceding edge, viewed in reverse; it can equal $v_-$. The node $W$ lies in the open suffix of $U$ after $v_-$; Figure <a href="#fig:switch" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:switch">3</a> shows this arrangement.

<a id="eq:G"></a>
Let $H_-$ be the low set of the preceding edge in its old working frame, regarded as a subset of all of $U$. The extension $H_-^*$ from [lem:old-low](#lem:old-low) is an upset in the current, reversed frame. Define $$G=D_{a_-}\cap(J\setminus\widehat P_{a})
            \cap(J\setminus\widehat P_{v_-})\cap H_-^* .
 \label{eq:G}$$

<a id="stmt-lem-g"></a>
<div id="lem:G" class="lemma">

**Lemma 21**. *The set $G$ in <a href="#eq:G" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:G">[eq:G]</a> is a global upset satisfying both properties in <a href="#eq:G-properties" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:G-properties">[eq:G-properties]</a>.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-g"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-g</summary>

*Proof.* All four factors in <a href="#eq:G" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:G">[eq:G]</a> are global upsets in the current frame, so $G$ is an upset.

Let $x\in X=W\setminus D_a$. If $x$ had been high at the preceding edge in its old frame, [lem:edge-labels](#lem:edge-labels) would put it in the old predecessor cone of that edge’s ending endpoint. That endpoint is the current $a$, so reversal would give $x\in D_a$, a contradiction. Thus $x\in H_-$. Every job of $H_-$ precedes the old right boundary of $U$, because all edge cutoffs lie in that cone. Equivalently, $H_-\subseteq D_{a_-}$ in the current frame. Since $x$ is strictly after both $a$ and $v_-$, feasibility excludes $x$ from their weak predecessor cones. Finally, $x\in U$, so agreement of $H_-^*$ on $U$ gives $x\in H_-^*$. Hence $X\subseteq G$.

Conversely, suppose $z\in G$ is scheduled at or before $a$. Membership in $D_{a_-}$, together with <a href="#eq:ancestry" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ancestry">[eq:ancestry]</a>, first places it strictly inside $U$: <a id="eq:localize-U"></a>$$a_-<\operatorname{time}(z)\le a<b\le b_-.
 \label{eq:localize-U}$$ Only now use $H_-^*\cap U=H_-$. The old edge cutoff $H_-$ was contained in the old center-low set. If $z$ is at or before $v_-$ in the current frame, the old center separator puts it in current $\widehat P_{v_-}$. If it is strictly after $v_-$ but at or before $a$, it lies in the old normalized side and the old separator at the endpoint $a$ puts it in current $\widehat P_{a}$. Both possibilities contradict <a href="#eq:G" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:G">[eq:G]</a>.

These separator implications remain valid when preparing the current node: all intervening reorderings have been confined to $W=(a,b)$, and therefore have not changed any job at or before $a$ in the current frame, the relevant boundary triples, or the fixed old cutoff sets. ◻

</details>

<figure id="fig:switch">

<figcaption>The ancestor configuration at a direction change, in the new working frame. A candidate early member of $G$ is first located inside $U$ by $D_{a−}$; only there is the earlier cutoff extension used. The two weak predecessor exclusions then cover the portions at or before $v_{−}$ and between $v_{−}$ and $a$. The equality $a = v_{−}$ is allowed; the intervening segment then collapses. The horizontal arrow denotes time.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-switch"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:switch`)

    {\draw[thick] (\x,-.18) -- (\x,.65);}

<a id="pm-h-22"></a>
#### Predicate bounds and the induction order

<a id="stmt-lem-entry-size"></a>
<div id="lem:entry-size" class="lemma">

**Lemma 22**. *Every entry constructed above has fewer than $40$ leaves. This bound persists through any number of same-direction updates.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-entry-size"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-entry-size</summary>

*Proof.* Root bases, injections, and second switch bases are simple predicates. By [lem:old-low](#lem:old-low), the $H_-^*$ factor in a new $G$ has at most $21$ leaves. The three other factors of $G$ and its outer rank suffix use four more. Thus a new first switch entry has at most $25$ leaves. Further same-direction trimming only tightens that outer rank suffix. It does not append a new independent condition. The same observation applies to simple entries. ◻

</details>

For completeness, the construction so far has a well-founded dependency order. Assume the parent and ancestor data already satisfy their assertions. A same update is defined directly. A switch update uses the preceding edge’s low set; [lem:old-low](#lem:old-low) derives its short extension from already existing lists and, when necessary, an earlier switch’s covering property. [lem:G](#lem:G) then proves the new covering and early-exclusion properties. [lem:update-labels](#lem:update-labels) supplies the high-set identity and Invariant <a href="#inv:cones" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inv:cones">[inv:cones]</a>; supplies the new length bound; and [lem:entry-size](#lem:entry-size) supplies the predicate bound. The length argument needs global closure and high-set identities, but not the numerical predicate bound. Thus neither bound assumes itself at a deeper node.

The root starts this induction. We next prove the remaining boundary invariants for every update, using only the parent invariants and the current normalizations.

<a id="sec:boundary"></a>
### Preserving the global boundary invariants

We verify Invariants <a href="#inv:past" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inv:past">[inv:past]</a> and <a href="#inv:swap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inv:swap">[inv:swap]</a> for a child $W'=(a',b')$ of $W=(a,b)$. All notation is in the working frame of the child edge. In a same update this is also the parent’s tag frame. In a switch update the old list is not carried; the newly constructed upsets are used instead.

Preparatory changes to the schedule of $W$ preserve the parent invariants. Its boundary triples and all earlier exterior jobs stay fixed, so Invariant <a href="#inv:past" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inv:past">[inv:past]</a> is unchanged. Invariant <a href="#inv:swap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inv:swap">[inv:swap]</a> already quantifies over every feasible reordering of $W$.

<a id="pm-h-24"></a>
#### Excluding distant qualifiers

<a id="stmt-lem-past"></a>
<div id="lem:past" class="lemma">

**Lemma 23**. *The updated child list satisfies Invariant <a href="#inv:past" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inv:past">[inv:past]</a>.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-past"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-past</summary>

*Proof.* It suffices to prove the assertion before pruning, since pruning only removes qualifiers. Let $z$ qualify for the pre-pruning child list and be scheduled at or before $a'$.

If $a<\operatorname{time}(z)\le a'$, the job belongs to the normalized open side $E$ of the parent. By [lem:update-labels](#lem:update-labels), it is high at the edge cutoff $S$. The local separator at $a'$ puts it in $P_{a'}$ when strictly earlier, and membership in the triple puts it in $\widehat P_{a'}$ when its time equals $a'$.

Now suppose $z$ is at or before $a$. The new injection $D_a\setminus P_b$ cannot contain such a job. Nor can either switch entry, by [lem:G](#lem:G) and the strictness of $D_a$. Thus the only remaining case is a same-direction carry qualifier. The parent’s Invariant <a href="#inv:past" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inv:past">[inv:past]</a> supplies a job $y\in a$ with $z=y$ or $z\prec y$. The carry is a global upset, so $y$ also qualifies by carry. In particular $a$ is actual. If $a'=a$, we are done.

Assume $a'>a$, and write $a'=s_i$, with edge cutoff $S=S_i$. Follow all the strict advances of the walk from $a$ through promotion $i$, including intermediate advances not retained as marks. Every cutoff used in this portion of the walk is contained in $S_i$. On $W$, the final carry is exactly $O\setminus S_i$. Consequently every carry qualifier in $E$ is high, and has worse priority than the low exceptions, at every one of these advances.

The first advance needs the parent exchange invariant, because $a$ lies outside the normalized open side $E$. Consider that first actual advance off $a$, with earliest exception $x$ at its target $t$. All *global* predecessors of $x$ are before $a$. To verify this stronger readiness assertion, every predecessor is temporally before $t$. If one were at $a$, then $x\in D_a$. If one were strictly between $a$ and $t$, it would belong to the contiguous side $E$, be low, and descend from $a$ by earliestness of the exception. Again $x\in D_a$, a contradiction. There is no other possible predecessor at or after $a$.

Also $x\in X=W\setminus D_a$, so Invariant <a href="#inv:cones" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inv:cones">[inv:cones]</a> puts it among the old qualifiers, outside the starting low set. It has already been promoted by stage $i$, so its old key is strictly below the least unpromoted key at $S_i$. By [lem:carry](#lem:carry), the boundary job $y$ has old key at least that least unpromoted key. The parent’s Invariant <a href="#inv:swap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inv:swap">[inv:swap]</a>, applied after the current preparatory reorderings of $W$, therefore prohibits exchanging $x$ and $y$ in the complete schedule.

If $y$ had no global successor at or before $t$, that exchange would be feasible. All global predecessors of $x$ lie strictly before $a$; moving it earlier preserves its successor constraints, and delaying $y$ to $t$ preserves its predecessor constraints and, under the supposition, its successor constraints. Thus $y$ must have a strict successor by $t$. Such a successor is in $E$ and is still a carry qualifier.

Any carry job strictly between $a$ and $t$ must likewise have a successor by $t$: otherwise exchange it with $x$ within $E$, contradicting its lexicographic normalization. The carry is an upset, so these successors remain in the carry. Iterating along strictly increasing times reaches a carry job at $t$. It cannot be $x$, which is low and lies outside the final carry.

At every subsequent advance, the local readiness and exchange argument from [lem:shared](#lem:shared) applies inside $E$. All carry jobs remain high at that advance, so each carry job at its starting slot has a carry descendant at its target. Tracing from the descendant of $y$ at the first target eventually gives a descendant of $y$ at $a'$. Hence $z\in\widehat P_{a'}$, as required. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-25"></a>
#### Exchanges after future child reorderings

<a id="stmt-lem-swap"></a>
<div id="lem:swap" class="lemma">

**Lemma 24**. *The pruned child list satisfies Invariant <a href="#inv:swap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inv:swap">[inv:swap]</a>, with its quantifier over all feasible reorderings of $W'$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-swap"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-swap</summary>

*Proof.* Take any feasible reordering of $W'$ in its slots. Suppose $x\in W'\setminus D_{a'}$, that $y\in a'$ qualifies for the child list, and that the new key of $x$ is smaller than the new key of $y$. Invariant <a href="#inv:cones" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inv:cones">[inv:cones]</a>, already proved for the child in [lem:update-labels](#lem:update-labels), ensures that $x$ qualifies. By [lem:prune](#lem:prune), the same comparison holds in the new list before pruning.

First let $a'>a$. Both $x$ and $y$ are in the parent’s normalized side $E$, and both are high at its edge cutoff $S$, by [lem:update-labels](#lem:update-labels). The pre-pruning key order agrees with the priority order used to normalize $E$. Thus $x$ has better priority than $y$. The child reordering changes only slots strictly after $a'$. A feasible exchange of $x,y$ in the complete schedule would therefore give a feasible full schedule of the same $E$ with a better prefix at $a'$, contrary to [lem:lex-prefix](#lem:lex-prefix).

Now let $a'=a$. In the switch case there is no new qualifier at $a$, so no such $y$ exists. In the same case neither $x$ nor $y$ belongs to the new injection: $x\notin D_a$, and no job at $a$ is a strict descendant of $a$. Their pre-pruning comparison therefore uses only carry entries. By [lem:carry](#lem:carry), it implies the same old key comparison in the parent’s list. Moreover $x\in W\setminus D_a$. The preparatory reorderings and the arbitrary subsequent reordering of $W'$ together form a feasible reordering confined to $W$. The parent’s Invariant <a href="#inv:swap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inv:swap">[inv:swap]</a> rules out the proposed exchange. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-prop-construction-valid"></a>
<div id="prop:construction-valid" class="proposition">

**Proposition 25**. *Starting from any feasible full schedule, the marking and list construction is defined at every node. Its pruned lists are global upset lists of length at most five, with fewer than $40$ leaves per entry, and satisfy Invariants <a href="#inv:cones" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inv:cones">[inv:cones]</a>–<a href="#inv:swap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inv:swap">[inv:swap]</a>. Every branch terminates.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-construction-valid"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-construction-valid</summary>

*Proof.* Proceed by depth from the root data of [sec:construction](#sec:construction). Given valid ancestor and parent data, the center and the two normalized sides exist by [cor:any-ideal](#cor:any-ideal), [lem:reorder](#lem:reorder). The shared walk and [lem:edge-labels](#lem:edge-labels) define the children. For a same update the new list is direct. For a switch, [lem:old-low](#lem:old-low), [lem:G](#lem:G) construct the needed global upset using only earlier data, as explained at the end of [sec:lists](#sec:lists). Then [lem:update-labels](#lem:update-labels), [lem:list-length](#lem:list-length), [lem:entry-size](#lem:entry-size) give the first invariant and the bounds. [lem:past](#lem:past), [lem:swap](#lem:swap) give the other two invariants without using a claim about any deeper child. This completes the simultaneous induction. Every child omits the center, so its size decreases strictly. ◻

</details>

Invariant <a href="#inv:past" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inv:past">[inv:past]</a> will let a bounded predicate distinguish the child from qualifiers arbitrarily far in the past. Invariant <a href="#inv:swap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inv:swap">[inv:swap]</a> is what allows that first invariant to continue propagating after recursive reorderings. Neither is replaced by an assertion only about local separators.

<a id="sec:descriptions"></a>
### Global interval predicates and completion

We now describe each child as a global set without intersecting with a recursively described parent. This is the step that converts the construction into a polynomial family of subproblems.

<a id="pm-h-27"></a>
#### Global tests for local cutoffs

The cutoff predicates in this section are used only in the final interval and split formulas. The list construction and its entry-size bound are now complete, so these formulas may retain the existing bounded entries. The historical extension in [lem:old-low](#lem:old-low), by contrast, had to flatten an earlier run before a new switch entry could be constructed.

<a id="eq:cutoff-agreement"></a>
<a id="eq:direct-same-cutoff"></a>
For every walk cutoff $S$ in a parent $W$, choose a global predicate $\widetilde S$ satisfying $$\widetilde S\cap W=S.
 \label{eq:cutoff-agreement}$$ In the same case, let $\mathbf K_{\mathrm{carry}}$ be the carry determined by $O\setminus S$, before the new injection. Use $$\widetilde S=P_b\setminus\mathcal Q(\mathbf K_{\mathrm{carry}}).
 \label{eq:direct-same-cutoff}$$ On $W$, the carry selects precisely $O\setminus S$, while the low jobs are the initial $(W\setminus O)\cap P_b$ and the promoted portion of $O$. Thus <a href="#eq:cutoff-agreement" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cutoff-agreement">[eq:cutoff-agreement]</a> holds. In the switch case use <a id="eq:direct-switch-cutoff"></a>$$\widetilde S=(J\setminus D_a)\cap\{\lambda\le r\},
 \label{eq:direct-switch-cutoff}$$ where $r$ is the rank of the last promoted member of $X$, or $r=0$ before any promotion. These formulas are available at every walk cutoff, whether or not the associated open gap is nonempty. They are not required to have any prescribed meaning outside $W$.

<a id="pm-h-28"></a>
#### A child as a global set

Figure <a href="#fig:gap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:gap">4</a> summarizes the localization steps in the following formulas.

<a id="stmt-lem-global-child"></a>
<div id="lem:global-child" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:global-high"></a>
<a id="eq:global-low"></a>
**Lemma 26**. *In the valid construction of [prop:construction-valid](#prop:construction-valid), let $W'=(a',b')$ be a child of $W=(a,b)$, with parent center $v$, edge cutoff $S$, new pruned list $\mathbf K'$, and cutoff predicate $\widetilde S$ satisfying <a href="#eq:cutoff-agreement" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cutoff-agreement">[eq:cutoff-agreement]</a>. In the edge’s working frame, its high and low parts are the following global sets: $$\begin{aligned}
 W'\setminus S
 &=\mathcal Q(\mathbf K')\cap P_{b'}\cap(J\setminus\widehat P_{a'}),
 \label{eq:global-high}\\
 W'\cap S
 &=D_{a'}\cap P_b\cap(J\setminus\widehat D_{b'})
                  \cap(J\setminus\widehat D_{v})\cap\widetilde S .
 \label{eq:global-low}
\end{aligned}$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-global-child"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-global-child</summary>

*Proof.* Every high child job qualifies, precedes $b'$, and, because it is later than $a'$, is outside $\widehat P_{a'}$. Conversely, a job selected by the right side of <a href="#eq:global-high" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:global-high">[eq:global-high]</a> is strictly before $b'$ by precedence. If it were at or before $a'$, the child’s global Invariant <a href="#inv:past" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="inv:past">[inv:past]</a> would put it in $\widehat P_{a'}$. It is therefore strictly in $W'$, and the child list selects exactly its high jobs.

For <a href="#eq:global-low" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:global-low">[eq:global-low]</a>, consider first a job selected by its right side. The two positive tests $D_{a'}\cap P_b$ locate it strictly inside the parent $W$: its time is after $a'\ge a$ and before $b$. This remains true with sentinel boundaries under our conventions. We can consequently use <a href="#eq:cutoff-agreement" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cutoff-agreement">[eq:cutoff-agreement]</a> to infer that the job is in $S$. This cutoff is contained in the center-low set $S_*$. A selected job at or after $v$ would therefore lie in $\widehat D_{v}$, by the center separator or by membership in $v$ itself. The corresponding exclusion places it in the open side $E=(a,v)$. Within $E$, a low job at or after $b'$ is in $\widehat D_{b'}$, using the local separator at $b'$. The remaining exclusion puts the selected job strictly between $a'$ and $b'$.

Conversely, a low job of this gap lies in $D_{a'}\cap P_b$ by [lem:edge-labels](#lem:edge-labels), and it satisfies $\widetilde S$ by <a href="#eq:cutoff-agreement" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cutoff-agreement">[eq:cutoff-agreement]</a>. Its time is strictly before both $b'$ and $v$, so it is in neither of their weak successor cones. It passes every test in <a href="#eq:global-low" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:global-low">[eq:global-low]</a>. ◻

</details>

<figure id="fig:gap">

<figcaption>The two global tests for a child. For the low part, positive cones locate a candidate inside the parent before its cutoff predicate is used; the two weak successor exclusions then restrict that candidate to the gap. For the high part, the global past-boundary invariant excludes qualifiers at or before $a′$. The intersection factors are arranged in the order used by the proof.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-gap"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:gap`)

Visual witness description of PDF page 19 (generated from the PDF page, not source text):

```text
A diagram displaying two structured set intersection formulas with explanatory labels:
1. "High jobs":
   - Formula: $\left( \bigcup_j K'_j \right) \cap P_{b'} \cap (J \setminus \widehat{P}_{a'}) = W' \setminus S$
   - Individual boxes surround $\bigcup_j K'_j$, $P_{b'}$, and $J \setminus \widehat{P}_{a'}$.
   - Below $J \setminus \widehat{P}_{a'}$, a bracketed label reads: "boundary invariant excludes early qualifiers".
2. "Low jobs":
   - Formula: $(D_{a'} \cap P_b) \cap \widetilde{S} \cap (J \setminus \widehat{D}_v) \cap (J \setminus \widehat{D}_{b'}) = W' \cap S$
   - Individual boxes surround each of the four intersected components, with labels below:
     - Under $D_{a'} \cap P_b$: "localize inside W"
     - Under $\widetilde{S}$: "cutoff test valid on W"
     - Under $J \setminus \widehat{D}_v$: "center separator: before v"
     - Under $J \setminus \widehat{D}_{b'}$: "edge separator: before $b'$"
```


The localization order matters. Neither the cutoff extension $\widetilde S$ nor the flattened historical extension needs to agree with its intended local set everywhere in $J$. Positive cone tests first place the candidate in the domain where agreement is known. The construction never appends a membership predicate for all ancestor intervals.

<a id="pm-h-29"></a>
#### Relative predicates for the marked splits

<a id="stmt-lem-relative-splits"></a>
<div id="lem:relative-splits" class="lemma">

**Lemma 27**. *Every marked slot $z$ in a node $W$ has a bounded relative description of its earlier jobs in either the working direction that produced it or the original direction. The formulas use the stated atomic language and have fewer than $500$ leaves, as counted in [lem:final-count](#lem:final-count).*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-relative-splits"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-relative-splits</summary>

*Proof.* Use the working frame for a side, and put $$H_*=
 \begin{cases}
 P_b,&\mathsf{same},\\
 J\setminus D_a,&\mathsf{switch}.
 \end{cases}$$ For the center $v$, the predicate $B_v(H_*)$, restricted to $W$, is its earlier set, by [lem:separator-description](#lem:separator-description) and <a href="#eq:center-cutoff" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:center-cutoff">[eq:center-cutoff]</a>.

<a id="eq:relative-split"></a>
For an internal mark $z$ in the open side $E$, choose a walk cutoff $S$ for which it is a local separator. The predicate $$F_z=B_v(H_*)\cap B_z(\widetilde S)
 \label{eq:relative-split}$$ gives its earlier set upon restriction to $W$. The first factor selects $E$ on $W$; on that domain the second is the separator description from [lem:separator-description](#lem:separator-description).

<a id="eq:reverse-split"></a>
If the working frame is opposite to original time, $F_z\cap W$ describes the original *later* jobs. Replace $F_z$ by $$(J\setminus F_z)\setminus z
 \label{eq:reverse-split}$$ to obtain the original earlier jobs after restriction to $W$. The exclusion of the marked triple is necessary: complementation alone includes it. Apply the same conversion to the center predicate when appropriate. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-lem-final-count"></a>
<div id="lem:final-count" class="lemma">

**Lemma 28**. *The predicates in [lem:global-child](#lem:global-child), [lem:relative-splits](#lem:relative-splits) have fewer than $500$ leaves.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-final-count"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-final-count</summary>

*Proof.* A pruned list or a carry contains at most five entries with fewer than $40$ leaves each, so its union has at most $195$ leaves. An empty union uses the false constant. The same-direction cutoff <a href="#eq:direct-same-cutoff" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:direct-same-cutoff">[eq:direct-same-cutoff]</a> therefore needs at most $196$ leaves; the switched one needs two. Use $205$ as a common loose allowance.

The high predicate <a href="#eq:global-high" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:global-high">[eq:global-high]</a> uses at most $195+2=197$ leaves. The low predicate <a href="#eq:global-low" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:global-low">[eq:global-low]</a> uses at most $205+4=209$. Their union is a global description of $W'$ with at most $406$ leaves.

The center predicate has three leaves. The internal split <a href="#eq:relative-split" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:relative-split">[eq:relative-split]</a> has at most $3+(205+2)=210$, and conversion <a href="#eq:reverse-split" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:reverse-split">[eq:reverse-split]</a> adds one. Complementation does not increase leaf counts. Every cone uses an actual triple or a sentinel constant; all ranks and thresholds are from the two allowed frames. Thus these are formulas in the stated atomic language. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-final-count-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-final-count</summary>

*Proof of [thm:structure](#thm:structure).* Use [prop:construction-valid](#prop:construction-valid) to construct marks and lists from a feasible full schedule. The root set $J$ is represented by true. Every nonempty child has the global description supplied by [lem:global-child](#lem:global-child). Every mark has the relative split description supplied by [lem:relative-splits](#lem:relative-splits). [lem:final-count](#lem:final-count) places both kinds of description in $\mathcal F$, independently of their depth. All branches terminate because the node sizes decrease strictly. These are exactly the conditions of [def:hierarchy](#def:hierarchy). ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:complexity"></a>
### Running time in the explicit input model

The preceding structural proof establishes completeness of the finite search. We now give the detailed deterministic multitape accounting promised in [prop:algorithm](#prop:algorithm). Its purpose is to make the polynomial-time assertion uniform; the structural argument does not require efficient normalization of a schedule or a small recursion depth.

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-final-count-3"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-final-count</summary>

*Running-time bound in [prop:algorithm](#prop:algorithm).* First specify a conservative implementation using sequential scans. Write $B=C_KN^{3K}$ for an upper bound on the number of enumerated formulas, where $C_K$ depends only on the fixed $K$. There are at most $B$ distinct sets and at most $S=BN^3$ candidate states for each $W$. Store their bit vectors, reachability marks, and predecessor record numbers on work tapes. Each state record uses $O_K(N)$ bits: its indices require only $O_K(\log N)$ bits. Store an explicit full schedule with each previously accepted set. This table uses $O_K(BN^2)$ bits, since a schedule lists at most $N$ job identifiers and their slots.

A complete scan to fetch or update a state record costs $O_K(SN)=O_K(BN^4)$ steps. A lookup in the acceptance table by comparing all bit vectors costs $O_K(BN^2)$ steps. Even checking all required precedence relations by repeated scans of the $N$-by-$N$ reachability table costs at most $O(N^4)$. Thus one ordered state-pair test and reachability update costs $O_K(BN^4)$ steps, without constant-time random access. Starting from the initial states, make at most $S$ passes over all ordered pairs, marking a target when its source is marked and <a href="#eq:transition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transition">[eq:transition]</a> holds. These passes find every reachable state. For all sets together their cost is at most $$O_K\bigl(BS^3BN^4\bigr)
 =O_K(B^5N^{13})
 =O_K(N^{15K+13}).$$ Generating, evaluating, and deduplicating formulas by scans costs at most $O_K(B^2N^4)$; reachability preprocessing, state construction, and table initialization also fit within the displayed bound. The subscript $K$ denotes a constant fixed independently of the input. Since $N\le3n$ and the vertices are explicitly listed, the looser bound $O((L+2)^{150020})$ covers input processing and even all $n$ deadline trials needed for optimization. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-rem-compact"></a>
<div id="rem:compact" class="remark">

*Remark 29*. For the deadline feasibility decision, the same preprocessing can handle an encoding that specifies an enormous number of isolated vertices only through a binary value of $n$. After checking $n\le3T$, let $d$ be the number of vertices appearing in edges. If $T\ge d$, accept: schedule those $d$ vertices sequentially in topological order and fill free positions with the isolated vertices. Otherwise $N=3T<3d\le6|E|$, so expansion is polynomial. This extra branch is not needed for the explicit encoding in [thm:main](#thm:main).

</div>

<a id="sec:conclusion"></a>
### Conclusion

The finite search succeeds because every interval in a suitable recursive schedule decomposition has a global description of bounded size, independent of its depth. Local separator descriptions alone do not give that guarantee: the short upset lists, the exterior boundary invariants, and the simplification at direction changes prevent the accumulation of ancestor information.

The resulting algorithm constructs an exact optimum for the stated three-machine model. The fixed leaf allowance $10000$ and the explicit large exponent establish polynomial time for the stated model. The proof supplies no practical performance guarantee.

      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[x=1cm,y=1cm,font=\\small,>=stealth]\n  \\fill[blue!7] (3.15,-.25) rectangle (6.35,.62);\n  \\draw[->,gray!75] (-.25,0) -- (10.6,0);\n  \\node[anchor=east,font=\\footnotesize] at (10.6,-.75)\n    {time in the working frame};\n  \\foreach \\x in {0,3,6.5,9.6}\n    {\\draw[thick] (\\x,-.18) -- (\\x,.62);}\n  \\node[above] at (0,.62) {$a=s_0$};\n  \\node[above] at (3,.62) {$a'=s_i$};\n  \\node[above] at (6.5,.62) {$b'=s_{i+1}$};\n  \\node[above] at (9.6,.62) {$v=s_{q+1}$};\n  \\node at (4.75,.22) {$W'=(a',b')$};\n  \\node at (1.5,.22) {$\\cdots$};\n  \\node at (8.05,.22) {$\\cdots$};\n  \\draw[blue!65!black] (3,1.18) -- (3,1.4) -- (6.5,1.4) -- (6.5,1.18);\n  \\node[above,text=blue!65!black] at (4.75,1.4)\n    {both endpoints separate at cutoff $S_i$};\n  \\node[anchor=north,align=center] at (4.75,-1.05)\n    {$W'\\cap S_i\\subseteq D_{a'}$\\qquad\n     $W'\\setminus S_i\\subseteq P_{b'}$};\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "A timeline diagram showing one side of a node in its working frame. At the bottom, a horizontal arrow pointing to the right is labeled \"time in the working frame\".       "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[x=1cm,y=1cm,font=\\small,>=stealth,\n  job/.style={circle,draw,fill=white,minimum size=6.8mm,inner sep=0pt}]\n  \\draw[black!55,rounded corners=2pt] (-.15,-.25) rectangle (10.75,2.85);\n  \\node[anchor=west] at (-.15,3.13) {Parent $W$};\n  \\node at (4,3.13) {Child $W'$};\n  \\node at (9.1,3.13) {$b'\\subset W$, $|b'|=3$};\n\n  \\fill[black!5] (8.55,-.12) rectangle (9.65,2.72);\n  \\foreach \\yy/\\ii/\\xx/\\cc in {2.2/1/3.55/blue!65!black,1.3/2/4/teal!65!black,.4/3/4.45/violet!70!black} {\n    \\draw[draw=\\cc,fill=\\cc!5,rounded corners=3pt]\n      (.03,\\yy-.34) rectangle (10.57,\\yy+.34);\n    \\node[text=\\cc] at (1.05,\\yy) {$I_{\\ii}\\cap W$};\n    \\node[text=\\cc] at (2.5,\\yy) {$\\cdots$};\n    \\node[job,draw=\\cc] (x\\ii) at (\\xx,\\yy) {$x_{\\ii}$};\n    \\node[job,draw=\\cc] (z\\ii) at (9.1,\\yy) {$z_{\\ii}$};\n    \\draw[->,draw=\\cc] (x\\ii) -- node[above,font=\\footnotesize] {$\\prec$} (z\\ii);\n    \\node[text=\\cc] at (10.05,\\yy) {$\\cdots$};\n  }\n  \\draw[dashed,black!60,rounded corners=2pt] (3.03,-.12) rectangle (4.98,2.72);\n  \\draw[black!60,rounded corners=2pt] (8.55,-.12) rectangle (9.65,2.72);\n  \\node[anchor=west,font=\\footnotesize] at (-.15,-.64)\n    {$x_i\\in I_i,\\ x_i\\prec z_i\\ \\Longrightarrow\\ z_i\\in I_i$\n     };\n  \\node[anchor=east,font=\\footnotesize] at (10.75,-.64)\n    {$I_i\\cap I_j\\cap W=\\varnothing\\ (i\\ne j)\\ \\Longrightarrow\\ z_i\\ne z_j$};\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "A schematic diagram illustrating three surviving injections. At the top, column regions are labeled: \"Parent $W$\", \"Child $W'$\", and \"$b' \\subset W, |b'| = 3$\".\nThree horizontal rows represent three injections $I_1, I_2, I_3$:\n- Row 1: A rounded rectangle labeled $I_1 \\cap W$, followed by dots $\\dots$, connecting to a circular node $x_1$, which connects via an arrow labeled $\\prec$ to a circular node $z_1$, followed by dots $\\dots$.\n- Row 2: A rounded rectangle labeled $I_2 \\cap W$, followed by dots $\\dots$, connecting to a circular node $x_2$, which connects via an arrow labeled $\\prec$ to a circular node $z_2$, followed by dots $\\dots$.\n- Row 3: A rounded rectangle labeled $I_3 \\cap W$, followed by dots $\\dots$, connecting to a circular node $x_3$, which connects via an arrow labeled $\\prec$ to a circular node $z_3$, followed by dots $\\dots$.\nA vertical rounded container encloses $\\{x_1, x_2, x_3\\}$ labeled \"Child $W'$\".\nA vertical rounded container encloses $\\{z_1, z_2, z_3\\}$ labeled \"$b' \\subset W, |b'| = 3$\".\nA larger rounded box labeled \"Parent $W$\" encompasses the sets $I_i \\cap W$.\nBeneath the diagram, two implications are stated:\n- $x_i \\in I_i, x_i \\prec z_i \\implies z_i \\in I_i$\n- $I_i \\cap I_j \\cap W = \\emptyset\\ (i \\neq j) \\implies z_i \\neq z_j$"

      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[x=1cm,y=1cm,font=\\small,>=stealth]\n  \\fill[teal!8] (4.35,-.25) rectangle (7.25,.65);\n  \\draw[->,gray!75] (-.25,0) -- (10.6,0);\n  \\foreach \\x in {0,2.2,4.2,7.4,9.6}\n    {\\draw[thick] (\\x,-.18) -- (\\x,.65);}\n  \\node[above] at (0,.65) {$a_-$};\n  \\node[above] at (2.2,.65) {$v_-$};\n  \\node[above] at (4.2,.65) {$a$};\n  \\node[above] at (7.4,.65) {$b$};\n  \\node[above] at (9.6,.65) {$b_-$};\n  \\node at (5.8,.25) {$W=(a,b)$};\n  \\draw[gray] (0,1.24) -- (0,1.43) -- (9.6,1.43) -- (9.6,1.24);\n  \\node[above] at (4.8,1.43) {$U=(a_-,b_-)$};\n  \\node[anchor=east,font=\\footnotesize] at (10.6,-.7)\n    {time in the new frame};\n  \\node[anchor=north,align=center] at (4.8,-1.05)\n    {$G=D_{a_-}\\cap (J\\setminus\\widehat P_a)\n      \\cap (J\\setminus\\widehat P_{v_-})\\cap H_-^*$};\n  \\node[draw=teal!60!black,fill=teal!3,rounded corners=2pt,\n        inner sep=8pt,align=center,font=\\footnotesize] at (4.8,-2.33)\n    {If $z\\in G$ were at or before $a$, then $z\\in U$ and hence $z\\in H_-$.\\\\[3pt]\n     At or before $v_-$: $z\\in\\widehat P_{v_-}$.\n     \\qquad After $v_-$ through $a$: $z\\in\\widehat P_a$.};\n\\end{tikzpicture}",

      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[x=1cm,y=1cm,font=\\small,\n  filter/.style={draw,rounded corners=2pt,minimum height=.75cm,\n                 minimum width=1.8cm,inner sep=4pt,align=center}]\n  \\node[anchor=west,font=\\bfseries] at (-.92,2.18) {High jobs};\n  \\node[filter,draw=blue!60!black,fill=blue!5] (h1) at (0,1.35)\n    {$\\displaystyle\\bigcup_j K'_j$};\n  \\node[filter,draw=blue!60!black,fill=blue!5] (h2) at (2.35,1.35)\n    {$P_{b'}$};\n  \\node[filter,draw=blue!60!black,fill=blue!5,minimum width=2.15cm] (h3) at (4.9,1.35)\n    {$J\\setminus\\widehat P_{a'}$};\n  \\node at (1.175,1.35) {$\\cap$};\n  \\node at (3.54,1.35) {$\\cap$};\n  \\node[anchor=west] at (6.32,1.35) {$=\\ W'\\setminus S$};\n  \\node[font=\\footnotesize,align=center] at (4.9,.49)\n    {boundary invariant\\\\excludes early qualifiers};\n\n  \\node[anchor=west,font=\\bfseries] at (-.92,-.36) {Low jobs};\n  \\node[filter,draw=teal!65!black,fill=teal!5,minimum width=2.15cm] (l1) at (.15,-1.18)\n    {$D_{a'}\\cap P_b$};\n  \\node[filter,draw=teal!65!black,fill=teal!5,minimum width=1.4cm] (l2) at (2.5,-1.18)\n    {$\\widetilde S$};\n  \\node[filter,draw=teal!65!black,fill=teal!5,minimum width=2.15cm] (l3) at (4.85,-1.18)\n    {$J\\setminus\\widehat D_v$};\n  \\node[filter,draw=teal!65!black,fill=teal!5,minimum width=2.15cm] (l4) at (7.55,-1.18)\n    {$J\\setminus\\widehat D_{b'}$};\n  \\node at (1.51,-1.18) {$\\cap$};\n  \\node at (3.49,-1.18) {$\\cap$};\n  \\node at (6.20,-1.18) {$\\cap$};\n  \\node[anchor=west] at (8.82,-1.18) {$=\\ W'\\cap S$};\n  \\node[font=\\footnotesize,align=center] at (.15,-2.08)\n    {localize\\\\inside $W$};\n  \\node[font=\\footnotesize,align=center] at (2.5,-2.08)\n    {cutoff test\\\\valid on $W$};\n  \\node[font=\\footnotesize,align=center] at (4.85,-2.08)\n    {center separator:\\\\before $v$};\n  \\node[font=\\footnotesize,align=center] at (7.55,-2.08)\n    {edge separator:\\\\before $b'$};\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
